<issue_description>
hatch fmt fails if [tool.ruff] exists in pyproject.toml
With hatch 1.8.0 (Python 3.11.6, Fedora 39), if there is a `[tool.ruff]` section in `pyproject.toml` then `hatch fmt` will fail:

```sh
$ hatch new foobar && cd foobar && echo '[tool.ruff]' >> pyproject.toml && hatch fmt
foobar
├── src
│   └── foobar
│       ├── __about__.py
│       └── __init__.py
├── tests
│   └── __init__.py
├── LICENSE.txt
├── README.md
└── pyproject.toml
ruff failed
  Cause: Failed to parse `<snip>/.local/share/hatch/env/.internal/fmt/.config/lC50A0FE/pyproject.toml`: TOML parse error at line 84, column 1
   |
84 | [tool.ruff]
   | ^
invalid table header
duplicate key `ruff` in table `tool`
```

The relevant portion of the auto-generated `pyproject.toml`:

```toml
[tool.ruff]

[tool.ruff]
extend = "<snip>/.local/share/hatch/env/.internal/fmt/.config/lC50A0FE/ruff_defaults.toml"
```

I think I would expect any settings that I have in _my_ `[tool.ruff]` to override Hatch's defaults. For example, if I have a `line-length = 100`, that would override the `line-length = 120` from Hatch's ruff defaults
</issue_description>

- The second one extends a defaults file

1.3 Problem explanation:
When a user has their own [tool.ruff] configuration in pyproject.toml and runs hatch fmt, Hatch internally generates a configuration file that merges the user's configuration with its own defaults. However, instead of properly merging the configurations, it's creating two separate [tool.ruff] sections, which violates TOML syntax rules (duplicate keys are not allowed).

1.4 Steps to reproduce:
1. Create a new hatch project: `hatch new foobar && cd foobar`
2. Add a [tool.ruff] section to pyproject.toml: `echo '[tool.ruff]' >> pyproject.toml`
3. Run hatch fmt: `hatch fmt`
4. Observe the TOML parsing error about duplicate keys

1.5 Best practices for fixing:
- Need to properly merge TOML configurations instead of duplicating sections
- User configurations should override defaults (as mentioned in the issue)
- Should preserve existing functionality while fixing the merge issue
- Need to handle various ruff configuration options properly</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 2.     81	            preview = self.linter_preview

   129	    def write_config_file(self, *, preview: bool | None) -> None:
   130	        config_contents = self.construct_config_file(preview=preview)
   131	        if self.config_path:

   160	
   161	    @cached_property
   162	    def user_config_file(self) -> Path | None:
   163	        # https://docs.astral.sh/ruff/configuration/#config-file-discovery
   164	        for possible_config in ('.ruff.toml', 'ruff.toml', 'pyproject.toml'):
   165	            if (config_file := (self.root / possible_config)).is_file():
   166	                return config_file
   167	
   168	        return None
   169	
   170	    @cached_property
   171	    def user_config(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
   172	        if self.user_config_file is None:
   173	            return {}
   174	
   175	        from hatch.utils.toml import load_toml_data
   176	
   177	        return load_toml_data(self.user_config_file.read_text())
   178	
   179	    @cached_property
   180	    def linter_preview(self) -> bool:
   181	        return self.get_config('lint').get('preview', False)
   182	
   183	    @cached_property
   184	    def formatter_preview(self) -> bool:
   185	        return self.get_config('format').get('preview', False)

    12	def construct_ruff_defaults_file(rules: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    13	    lines = [
    14	        'line-length = 120',
    15	        '',
    16	        '[format]',
    17	        'docstring-code-format = true',
    18	        'docstring-code-line-length = 80',
    19	        '',
    20	        '[lint]',
    21	    ]
    22	
    23	    # Selected rules
    24	    lines.append('select = [')
    25	    lines.extend(f'  "{rule}",' for rule in sorted(rules))
    26	    lines.extend((']', ''))
    27	
    28	    # Ignored rules
    29	    lines.append('[lint.per-file-ignores]')
    30	    for glob, ignored_rules in PER_FILE_IGNORED_RULES.items():
    31	        lines.append(f'"{glob}" = [')
    32	        lines.extend(f'  "{ignored_rule}",' for ignored_rule in ignored_rules)
    33	        lines.append(']')
    34	
    35	    # Default config
    36	    lines.extend((
    37	        '',
    38	        '[lint.flake8-tidy-imports]',
    39	        'ban-relative-imports = "all"',
    40	        '',
    41	        '[lint.isort]',
    42	        'known-first-party = ["my_app"]',
    43	        '',
    44	        '[lint.flake8-pytest-style]',
    45	        'fixture-parentheses = false',
    46	        'mark-parentheses = false',
    47	    ))
    48	
    49	    # Ensure the file ends with a newline to satisfy other linters
    50	    lines.append('')
    51	
    52	    return '\n'.join(lines)

## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating Reproduction Script<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8/reproduce_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Script to reproduce the hatch fmt issue with duplicate [tool.ruff] sections.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def reproduce_issue():
    """Reproduce the hatch fmt issue with duplicate [tool.ruff] sections."""
    print("Creating temporary directory...")
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        print(f"Working in: {temp_dir}")
        
        # Create a new hatch project
        print("Creating hatch project...")
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)

import tempfile

import sys
from pathlib import Path


def test_fix():
    """Test that hatch fmt works correctly with existing [tool.ruff] sections."""
    print("Creating temporary directory...")

        print(f"Working in: {temp_dir}")

        print("Creating hatch project...")
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print(f"Failed to create project: {result.stderr}")
            return False
            
        # Change to project directory
        os.chdir('test-project')
        
        # Add a [tool.ruff] section with custom configuration to pyproject.toml

        with open('pyproject.toml', 'a') as f:

            content = f.read()
            print(content)
            
        # Try to run hatch fmt - this should work after the fix
        print("\nRunning hatch fmt...")
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'fmt'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)

        print(f"Stdout: {result.stdout}")

                    return False

                return False

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly analyze the problem and solution:

5.1 What the problem is:
In src/hatch/env/internal/fmt.py, the write_config_file method (lines 145-152) blindly appends a new [tool.ruff] section to the user's pyproject.toml content when creating the internal configuration file. If the user already has a [tool.ruff] section, this creates duplicate keys in the TOML file, causing ruff to fail with a parsing error.

5.2 Where the problem is located:
File: src/hatch/env/internal/fmt.py
Method: write_config_file
Lines: 145-152, specifically line 148 where it does:
f'{old_contents}\n[tool.ruff]\nextend = "{config_path}"'

5.3 How the test reproduces the issue:
The test creates a pyproject.toml with an existing [tool.ruff] section, then runs hatch fmt. This triggers the write_config_file method which creates an internal pyproject.toml with two [tool.ruff] sections, causing ruff to fail.

5.4 Best practices to take into account:
- Need to properly parse and merge TOML content rather than simple string concatenation
- User configurations should override defaults (as mentioned in the issue)
- Should preserve existing functionality for cases where there's no [tool.ruff] section
- Need to handle various TOML structures and edge cases
- Should use proper TOML parsing libraries that are already available in the project

5.5 How to fix the problem:
Instead of blindly appending [tool.ruff], I need to:
1. Parse the existing user pyproject.toml content as TOML
2. Check if [tool.ruff] already exists
3. If it exists, modify the existing section to add the extend directive
4. If it doesn't exist, append the [tool.ruff] section with extend directive
5. Write the properly merged TOML back to the internal config file

Looking at the imports and existing code, I can see that hatch.utils.toml.load_toml_data is used elsewhere in the file, so I should use similar utilities for TOML manipulation.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me check what TOML utilities are available:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8 && find . -name "toml.py" -o -name "*toml*" | grep -v __pycache__ | grep utils</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>./src/hatch/utils/toml.py

        if self.internal_user_config_file is None:
            return

        if self.user_config_file is None:
            return

        old_contents = self.user_config_file.read_text()
        config_path = str(self.internal_config_file).replace('\\', '\\\\')
        
        if self.user_config_file.name == 'pyproject.toml':
            # Parse the existing TOML content
            import tomlkit
            doc = tomlkit.parse(old_contents)
            
            # Check if [tool.ruff] already exists
            if 'tool' in doc and 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
                # If it exists, add the extend property to the existing section
                doc['tool']['ruff']['extend'] = config_path
            else:
                # If it doesn't exist, create the section with extend property
                if 'tool' not in doc:
                    doc['tool'] = tomlkit.table()
                doc['tool']['ruff'] = {'extend': config_path}
            
            contents = tomlkit.dumps(doc)
        else:
            contents = f'extend = "{config_path}"\n{old_contents}'

# Test TOML parsing behavior
import tomlkit

def test_toml_parsing():
    """Test how tomlkit handles duplicate sections."""
    print("Testing TOML parsing behavior...")
    
    # Simulate the problematic pyproject.toml content
    sample_content = '''[project]
name = "test-project"

[tool.ruff]
line-length = 100
'''
    
    print("Original content:")
    print(sample_content)
    
    # Parse it
    doc = tomll.parse(sample_content)
    print("\nParsed document structure:")
    print(f"Has 'tool': {'tool' in doc}")
    if 'tool' in doc:
        print(f"Has 'ruff': {'ruff' in doc['tool']}")
        if 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
            print(f"Ruff section: {doc['tool']['ruff']}")
    
    # Try to add extend property
    config_path = "/some/path/ruff_defaults.toml"
    if 'tool' in doc and 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
        print(f"\nAdding extend property to existing ruff section...")
        doc['tool']['ruff']['extend'] = config_path
    else:
        print(f"\nCreating new ruff section...")
        if 'tool' not in doc:
            doc['tool'] = tomlkit.table()
        doc['tool']['ruff'] = {'extend': config_path}
    
    # Dump the result
    result = tomlkit.dumps(doc)
    print(f"\nResult:")
    print(result)
    
    # Count [tool.ruff] occurrences
    count = result.count('[tool.ruff]')
    print(f"\nNumber of [tool.ruff] sections: {count}")

Let me create a better debug script that replicates exactly what happens in the fmt.py file:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8/debug_fmt.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Debug script to replicate exactly what happens in fmt.py.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path
import tomlkit

def debug_fmt_processing():
    """Debug the exact processing that happens in fmt.py."""
    print("Debugging fmt.py processing...")
    
    # Create a temporary directory and project
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        print(f"Working in: {temp_dir}")
        
        # Create a new hatch project
        print("Creating hatch project...")
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print(f"Failed to create project: {result.stderr}")
            return False
            
        # Change to project directory
        os.chdir('test-project')
        
        # Add a [tool.ruff] section with custom configuration to pyproject.toml
        print("Adding [tool.ruff] section with custom config to pyproject.toml...")
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'a') as f:
            f.write('\n[tool.ruff]\nline-length = 100\n')
            
        # Read the content exactly as fmt.py would
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'r') as f:
            old_contents = f.read()
        
        print("\nOriginal pyproject.toml content:")
        print(repr(old_contents))
        print("\nOriginal pyproject.toml content (formatted):")
        print(old_contents)
        
        # Simulate what happens in write_config_file
        config_path = "/test/path/ruff_defaults.toml"
        
        print(f"\nProcessing with config_path: {config_path}")
        
        # Parse the existing TOML content
        doc = tomlkit.parse(old_contents)
        print(f"Parsed document keys: {list(doc.keys())}")
        
        if 'tool' in doc:
            print(f"'tool' section keys: {list(doc['tool'].keys())}")
            if 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
                print(f"'ruff' section content: {doc['tool']['ruff']}")
                print(f"'ruff' section type: {type(doc['tool']['ruff'])}")
        
        # Check if [tool.ruff] already exists
        if 'tool' in doc and 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
            print("Found existing [tool.ruff] section, adding extend property...")
            # If it exists, add the extend property to the existing section
            doc['tool']['ruff']['extend'] = config_path
        else:
            print("No existing [tool.ruff] section found, creating new one...")
            # If it doesn't exist, create the section with extend property
            if 'tool' not in doc:
                doc['tool'] = tomlkit.table()
            doc['tool']['ruff'] = {'extend': config_path}
        
        contents = tomlkit.dumps(doc)
        print(f"\nProcessed content:")
        print(contents)
        
        # Count [tool.ruff] occurrences
        count = contents.count('[tool.ruff]')
        print(f"\nNumber of [tool.ruff] sections: {count}")
        
        if count == 1:
            print("✓ Success! Only one [tool.ruff] section")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Failed! Found {count} [tool.ruff] sections")
            return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = debug_fmt_processing()
    sys.exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8/debug_fmt.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8 && python debug_fmt.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Debugging fmt.py processing...

"""
Check what's actually in the internal config file after hatch fmt runs.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def check_internal_config():
    """Check the internal config after hatch fmt."""
    print("Creating temporary directory...")
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        print(f"Working in: {temp_dir}")
        
        # Create a new hatch project
        print("Creating hatch project...")
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print(f"Failed to create project: {result.stderr}")
            return False
            
        # Change to project directory
        os.chdir('test-project')
        
        # Add a [tool.ruff] section with custom configuration to pyproject.toml
        print("Adding [tool.ruff] section with custom config to pyproject.toml...")
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'a') as f:
            f.write('\n[tool.ruff]\nline-length = 100\n')
            
        # Show the pyproject.toml content
        print("\nContent of pyproject.toml:")
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'r') as f:
            content = f.read()
            print(content)
            
        # Try to run hatch fmt
        print("\nRunning hatch fmt...")
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'fmt'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        
        print(f"Exit code: {result.returncode}")
        print(f"Stdout: {result.stdout}")
        print(f"Stderr: {result.stderr}")
        
        if result.returncode == 0:
            print("\n✓ hatch fmt succeeded")
            
            # Find the internal config directory
            config_dirs = list(Path.home().glob('.local/share/hatch/env/.internal/fmt/.config/*'))
            if config_dirs:
                config_dir = config_dirs[0]
                internal_pyproject = config_dir / 'pyproject.toml'
                if internal_pyproject.exists():
                    print(f"\nInternal pyproject.toml path: {internal_pyproject}")
                    
                    # Read the file in binary mode to see exactly what's there
                    with open(internal_pyproject, 'rb') as f:
                        raw_content = f.read()
                        print(f"Raw content length: {len(raw_content)} bytes")
                        print(f"Raw content: {repr(raw_content[:500])}")
                    
                    # Read normally
                    with open(internal_pyproject, 'r') as f:
                        content = f.read()
                        print(f"\nActual content:")
                        print(repr(content))
                        print(f"\nFormatted content:")
                        print(content)
                        
                        # Count occurrences
                        tool_ruff_count = content.count('[tool.ruff]')
                        print(f"\nNumber of [tool.ruff] sections: {tool_ruff_count}")
                        
                        if tool_ruff_count == 1:
                            print("✓ Success! Only one [tool.ruff] section")
                            return True
                        else:
                            print(f"✗ Still broken! Found {tool_ruff_count} [tool.ruff] sections")
                            return False
                else:
                    print("Could not find internal pyproject.toml")
                    return False
            else:
                print("Could not find config directory")
                return False
        else:
            print("\n✗ hatch fmt failed")
            return False


if __name__ == "__main__":
    success = check_internal_config()
    sys.exit(0 if success else 1)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8/check_internal_config.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think><tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8 && python check_internal_config.py</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Creating temporary directory...

"""
Debug the write_config_file method directly.
"""

# Let me manually test the exact logic from write_config_file

import tomlkit

def test_write_config_logic():
    """Test the exact logic from write_config_file."""
    
    # Simulate the original pyproject.toml content
    old_contents = '''[build-system]
requires = ["hatchling"]
build-backend = "hatchling.build"

[project]
name = "test-project"

[tool.ruff]
line-length = 100
'''

    config_path = "/test/path/ruff_defaults.toml"
    user_config_file_name = 'pyproject.toml'
    
    print("=== INPUT ===")
    print("old_contents:")
    print(repr(old_contents))
    print("config_path:", config_path)
    print()
    
    if user_config_file_name == 'pyproject.toml':
        # Parse the existing TOML content
        print("Parsing with tomlkit...")
        doc = tomlkit.parse(old_contents)
        print("Parsed document keys:", list(doc.keys()))
        if 'tool' in doc:
            print("'tool' section keys:", list(doc['tool'].keys()))
            if 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
                print("'ruff' section content:", doc['tool']['ruff'])
                print("'ruff' section type:", type(doc['tool']['ruff']))
        
        # Check if [tool.ruff] already exists
        if 'tool' in doc and 'ruff' in doc['tool']:
            print("Found existing [tool.ruff] section, adding extend property...")
            # If it exists, add the extend property to the existing section
            doc['tool']['ruff']['extend'] = config_path
        else:
            print("No existing [tool.ruff] section found, creating new one...")
            # If it doesn't exist, create the section with extend property
            if 'tool' not in doc:
                doc['tool'] = tomlkit.table()
            doc['tool']['ruff'] = {'extend': config_path}
        
        contents = tomlkit.dumps(doc)
        print("\n=== OUTPUT ===")
        print("contents:")
        print(repr(contents))
        print("\nFormatted contents:")
        print(contents)
        
        # Count [tool.ruff] occurrences
        count = contents.count('[tool.ruff]')
        print(f"\nNumber of [tool.ruff] sections: {count}")
        
        if count == 1:
            print("✓ Success! Only one [tool.ruff] section")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Failed! Found {count} [tool.ruff] sections")
            return False
    else:
        contents = f'extend = "{config_path}"\n{old_contents}'
        print("Non-pyproject.toml case")
        return True

if __name__ == "__main__":

"""
Test edge cases for the hatch fmt fix.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def test_case_1_empty_ruff_section():
    """Test with empty [tool.ruff] section."""
    print("=== Test Case 1: Empty [tool.ruff] section ===")
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        
        # Create a new hatch project
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print(f"Failed to create project: {result.stderr}")
            return False
            
        os.chdir('test-project')
        
        # Add an empty [tool.ruff] section
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'a') as f:
            f.write('\n[tool.ruff]\n')
            
        # Run hatch fmt
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'fmt'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        
        if result.returncode == 0:
            print("✓ Empty [tool.ruff] section handled correctly")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Failed: {result.stderr}")
            return False


def test_case_2_ruff_with_settings():
    """Test with [tool.ruff] section containing settings."""
    print("\n=== Test Case 2: [tool.ruff] with settings ===")
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        
        # Create a new hatch project
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print(f"Failed to create project: {result.stderr}")
            return False
            
        os.chdir('test-project')
        
        # Add [tool.ruff] section with multiple settings
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'a') as f:
            f.write('\n[tool.ruff]\nline-length = 100\nselect = ["E", "W"]\n')
            
        # Run hatch fmt
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'fmt'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        
        if result.returncode == 0:
            print("✓ [tool.ruff] with settings handled correctly")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Failed: {result.stderr}")
            return False


def test_case_3_no_ruff_section():
    """Test with no [tool.ruff] section (should still work)."""
    print("\n=== Test Case 3: No [tool.ruff] section ===")
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        
        # Create a new hatch project
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'test-project'],

        if result.returncode == 0:
            print("✓ No [tool.ruff] section handled correctly")
            return True
        else:
            print(f"✗ Failed: {result.stderr}")
            return False


def main():
    """Run all test cases."""
    os.chdir('/workspace/pypa__hatch__1.8')
    
    results = []
    results.append(test_case_1_empty_ruff_section())
    results.append(test_case_2_ruff_with_settings())
    results.append(test_case_3_no_ruff_section())
    
    passed = sum(results)
    total = len(results)
    
    print(f"\n=== SUMMARY ===")
    print(f"Passed: {passed}/{total}")
    
    if passed == total:
        print("✓ All edge cases passed!")
        return True
    else:
        print("✗ Some edge cases failed!")
        return False

"""
Debug the test failure to understand the exact format difference.
"""

import tempfile
import os
import subprocess
import sys
from pathlib import Path


def debug_test_format():
    """Debug what format the tests expect vs what we produce."""
    print("=== Debugging test format expectations ===")
    
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        os.chdir(temp_dir)
        
        # Create a new hatch project like the tests do
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'new', 'my-app'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        if result.returncode != 0:
            print(f"Failed to create project: {result.stderr}")
            return False
            
        os.chdir('my-app')
        
        # Show the original pyproject.toml content (like tests do)
        with open('pyproject.toml', 'r') as f:
            original_content = f.read()
        
        print("Original pyproject.toml content:")
        print(repr(original_content))
        print("\nFormatted:")
        print(original_content)
        
        # Run hatch fmt like the tests do
        result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-m', 'hatch', 'fmt'], 
                              capture_output=True, text=True)
        
        print(f"\nhatch fmt exit code: {result.returncode}")
        print(f"stdout: {result.stdout}")
        print(f"stderr: {result.stderr}")
        
        if result.returncode == 0:
            # Check what internal config was created
            config_dirs = list(Path.home().glob('.local/share/hatch/env/.internal/fmt/.config/*'))
            if config_dirs:
                config_dir = config_dirs[0]
                internal_pyproject = config_dir / 'pyproject.toml'
                if internal_pyproject.exists():
                    with open(internal_pyproject, 'r') as f:
                        actual_content = f.read()
                    
                    print(f"\nActual internal pyproject.toml content:")
                    print(repr(actual_content))
                    print("\nFormatted:")
                    print(actual_content)
                    
                    # What the tests expect (the old format)
                    config_path = str(config_dir / 'ruff_defaults.toml').replace('\\', '\\\\')
                    expected_old_format = f"{original_content}\n[tool.ruff]\nextend = \"{config_path}\""
                    
                    print(f"\nExpected old format would be:")
                    print(repr(expected_old_format))
                    print("\nFormatted:")
                    print(expected_old_format)
                    
                    print(f"\nAre they equal? {actual_content == expected_old_format}")
                    
                    # Count tool.ruff sections
                    actual_count = actual_content.count('[tool.ruff]')
                    expected_count = expected_old_format.count('[tool.ruff]')
                    print(f"Actual [tool.ruff] count: {actual_count}")
                    print(f"Expected [tool.ruff] count: {expected_count}")
                    
                    return True
                else:
                    print("No internal pyproject.toml found")
            else:
                print("No config dir found")
        else:
            print(f"hatch fmt failed: {result.stderr}")
        
        return False
